import abc
import logging
import os
import re
import string
import subprocess
import sys
import threading
import time

from pwnlib import atexit
from pwnlib import term
from pwnlib.context import context
from pwnlib.log import Logger
from pwnlib.timeout import Timeout
from pwnlib.tubes.buffer import Buffer
from pwnlib.util import fiddling
from pwnlib.util import iters
from pwnlib.util import misc
from pwnlib.util import packing


class tube(Timeout, Logger):
    """
    Container of all the tube functions common to sockets, TTYs and SSH connetions.
    """

    default = Timeout.default
    forever = Timeout.forever

    def __init__(self, timeout = default, level = None, *a, **kw):
        super(tube, self).__init__(timeout)

        Logger.__init__(self, None)
        if level is not None:
            self.setLevel(level)

        self.buffer = Buffer(*a, **kw)
        self._newline = None
        atexit.register(self.close)

    def _normalize_keepends_drop(self, keepends, drop, drop_default):
        '''
        >>> t = tube()
        >>> t._normalize_keepends_drop(None, None, True)
        True
        >>> t._normalize_keepends_drop(None, None, False)
        False
        >>> t._normalize_keepends_drop(None, True, True)
        True

END STEP

"""The pwnlib is not a big truck! It's a series of tubes!

This is our library for talking to sockets, processes, ssh connections etc.
Our goal is to be able to use the same API for e.g. remote TCP servers, local
TTY-programs and programs run over over SSH.

It is organized such that the majority of the functionality is implemented
in :class:`pwnlib.tubes.tube`. The remaining classes should only implement
just enough for the class to work and possibly code pertaining only to
that specific kind of tube.
"""
from pwnlib.tubes import listen
from pwnlib.tubes import process
from pwnlib.tubes import remote
from pwnlib.tubes import serialtube
from pwnlib.tubes import server
from pwnlib.tubes import sock
from pwnlib.tubes import ssh
from pwnlib.tubes import tube

__all__ = ['tube', 'sock', 'remote', 'listen', 'process', 'serialtube', 'server', 'ssh']

END STEP

returncode=0
     1	from pwnlib.context import context
     2	
     3	
     4	class Buffer(object):
     5	    """
     6	    List of strings with some helper routines.
     7	
     8	    Example:
     9	
    10	        >>> b = Buffer()
    11	        >>> b.add(b"A" * 10)
    12	        >>> b.add(b"B" * 10)
    13	        >>> len(b)
    14	        20
    15	        >>> b.get(1)
    16	        b'A'
    17	        >>> len(b)
    18	        19
    19	        >>> b.get(9999)
    20	        b'AAAAAAAAABBBBBBBBBB'
    21	        >>> len(b)
    22	        0
    23	        >>> b.get(1)
    24	        b''
    25	
    26	    Implementation Details:
    27	
    28	        Implemented as a list.  Strings are added onto the end.
    29	        The ``0th`` item in the buffer is the oldest item, and
    30	        will be received first.
    31	    """
    32	    def __init__(self, buffer_fill_size = None):
    33	        self.data = [] # Buffer
    34	        self.size = 0  # Length
    35	        self.buffer_fill_size = buffer_fill_size
    36	
    37	    def __len__(self):
    38	        """
    39	        >>> b = Buffer()
    40	        >>> b.add(b'lol')
    41	        >>> len(b) == 3
    42	        True
    43	        >>> b.add(b'foobar')
    44	        >>> len(b) == 9
    45	        True
    46	        """
    47	        return self.size
    48	
    49	    def __nonzero__(self):
    50	        return len(self) > 0
    51	
    52	    def __contains__(self, x):
    53	        """
    54	        >>> b = Buffer()
    55	        >>> b.add(b'asdf')
    56	        >>> b'x' in b
    57	        False
    58	        >>> b.add(b'x')
    59	        >>> b'x' in b
    60	        True
    61	        """
    62	        for b in self.data:
    63	            if x in b:
    64	                return True
    65	        return False
    66	
    67	    def index(self, x):
    68	        """
    69	        >>> b = Buffer()
    70	        >>> b.add(b'asdf')
    71	        >>> b.add(b'qwert')
    72	        >>> b.index(b't') == len(b) - 1
    73	        True
    74	        """
    75	        sofar = 0
    76	        for b in self.data:
    77	            if x in b:
    78	                return sofar + b.index(x)
    79	            sofar += len(b)
    80	        raise IndexError()
    81	
    82	    def add(self, data):
    83	        """
    84	        Adds data to the buffer.
    85	
    86	        Arguments:
    87	            data(str,Buffer): Data to add
    88	        """
    89	        # Fast path for ''
    90	        if not data: return
    91	
    92	        if isinstance(data, Buffer):
    93	            self.size += data.size
    94	            self.data += data.data
    95	        else:
    96	            self.size += len(data)
    97	            self.data.append(data)
    98	
    99	    def unget(self, data):
   100	        """
   101	        Places data at the front of the buffer.
   102	
   103	        Arguments:
   104	            data(str,Buffer): Data to place at the beginning of the buffer.
   105	
   106	        Example:
   107	
   108	            >>> b = Buffer()
   109	            >>> b.add(b"hello")
   110	            >>> b.add(b"world")
   111	            >>> b.get(5)
   112	            b'hello'
   113	            >>> b.unget(b"goodbye")
   114	            >>> b.get()
   115	            b'goodbyeworld'
   116	        """
   117	        if isinstance(data, Buffer):
   118	            self.data = data.data + self.data
   119	            self.size += data.size
   120	        else:
   121	            self.data.insert(0, data)
   122	            self.size += len(data)
   123	
   124	    def get(self, want=float('inf')):
   125	        """
   126	        Retrieves bytes from the buffer.
   127	
   128	        Arguments:
   129	            want(int): Maximum number of bytes to fetch
   130	
   131	        Returns:
   132	            Data as string
   133	
   134	        Example:
   135	
   136	            >>> b = Buffer()
   137	            >>> b.add(b'hello')
   138	            >>> b.add(b'world')
   139	            >>> b.get(1)
   140	            b'h'
   141	            >>> b.get()
   142	            b'elloworld'
   143	        """
   144	        # Fast path, get all of the data
   145	        if want >= self.size:
   146	            data   = b''.join(self.data)
   147	            self.size = 0
   148	            self.data = []
   149	            return data
   150	
   151	        # Slow path, find the correct-index chunk
   152	        have = 0
   153	        i    = 0
   154	        while want >= have:
   155	            have += len(self.data[i])
   156	            i    += 1
   157	
   158	        # Join the chunks, evict from the buffer
   159	        data   = b''.join(self.data[:i])
   160	        self.data = self.data[i:]
   161	
   162	        # If the last chunk puts us over the limit,
   163	        # stick the extra back at the beginning.
   164	        if have > want:
   165	            extra = data[want:]
   166	            data  = data[:want]
   167	            self.data.insert(0, extra)
   168	
   169	        # Size update
   170	        self.size -= len(data)
   171	
   172	        return data
   173	
   174	    def get_fill_size(self, size=None):
   175	        """
   176	        Retrieves the default fill size for this buffer class.
   177	
   178	        Arguments:
   179	            size (int): (Optional) If set and not None, returns the size variable back.
   180	
   181	        Returns:
   182	            Fill size as integer if size is None, else size.
   183	        """
   184	        if size is None:
   185	            size = self.buffer_fill_size
   186	
   187	        with context.local(buffer_size=size):
   188	            return context.buffer_size

END STEP

import socket
import socks

from pwnlib.log import getLogger
from pwnlib.timeout import Timeout
from pwnlib.tubes.sock import sock

log = getLogger(__name__)

class remote(sock):
    r"""Creates a TCP or UDP-connection to a remote host. It supports
    both IPv4 and IPv6.

    The returned object supports all the methods from
    :class:`pwnlib.tubes.sock` and :class:`pwnlib.tubes.tube`.

    Arguments:
        host(str): The host to connect to.
        port(int): The port to connect to.
        fam: The string "any", "ipv4" or "ipv6" or an integer to pass to :func:`socket.getaddrinfo`.
        typ: The string "tcp" or "udp" or an integer to pass to :func:`socket.getaddrinfo`.
        timeout: A positive number, None or the string "default".
        sock(:class:`socket.socket`): Socket to inherit, rather than connecting
        ssl(bool): Wrap the socket with SSL
        ssl_context(ssl.SSLContext): Specify SSLContext used to wrap the socket.
        ssl_args(dict): Pass :func:`ssl.wrap_socket` named arguments in a dictionary.
        sni(str,bool): Set 'server_hostname' in ssl_args. Set to True to set it based on the host argument. Set to False to not provide any value. Default is True.

    Examples:

        >>> r = remote('google.com', 443, ssl=True)
        >>> r.send(b'GET /\r\n\r\n')
        >>> r.recvn(4)
        b'HTTP'

        If a connection cannot be made, an exception is raised.

        >>> r = remote('127.0.0.1', 1)
        Traceback (most recent call last):
        ...
        PwnlibException: Could not connect to 127.0.0.1 on port 1

        You can also use :meth:`.remote.fromsocket` to wrap an existing socket.

        >>> import socket
        >>> s = socket.socket()
        >>> s.connect(('google.com', 80))
        >>> s.send(b'GET /' + b'\r\n'*2)
        9
        >>> r = remote.fromsocket(s)
        >>> r.recvn(4)
        b'HTTP'
        >>> s = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET6, socket.SOCK_STREAM) #doctest: +SKIP
        >>> s.connect(('2606:4700:4700::1111', 80)) #doctest: +SKIP
        >>> s.send(b'GET ' + b'\r\n'*2) #doctest: +SKIP
        8
        >>> r = remote.fromsocket(s) #doctest: +SKIP
        >>> r.recvn(4) #doctest: +SKIP
        b'HTTP'
    """

    def __init__(self, host, port,
                 fam = "any", typ = "tcp",
                 sock=None, ssl=False, ssl_context=None, ssl_args=None, sni=True,
                 *args, **kwargs):
        super(remote, self).__init__(*args, **kwargs)

        # convert port to string for sagemath support
        self.rport  = str(port)
        self.rhost  = host

        if sock:
            self.family = sock.family
            self.type   = sock.type
            self.proto  = sock.proto
            self.sock   = sock

        else:
            typ = self._get_type(typ)
            fam = self._get_family(fam)
            try:
                self.sock   = self._connect(fam, typ)
            except socket.gaierror as e:
                if e.errno != socket.EAI_NONAME:
                    raise
                self.exception('Could not resolve hostname: %r', host)
        if self.sock:
            self.settimeout(self.timeout)
            self.lhost, self.lport = self.sock.getsockname()[:2]

            if ssl:
                # Deferred import to save startup time
                import ssl as _ssl

                ssl_args = ssl_args or {}
                if "server_hostname" in ssl_args and sni:
                    log.error("sni and server_hostname cannot be set at the same time")
                ssl_context = ssl_context or _ssl.SSLContext(_ssl.PROTOCOL_TLSv1_2)
                if isinstance(sni, str):
                    ssl_args["server_hostname"] = sni
                elif sni:
                    ssl_args["server_hostname"] = host
                self.sock = ssl_context.wrap_socket(self.sock,**ssl_args)

    def _connect(self, fam, typ):
        sock    = None
        timeout = self.timeout

        with self.waitfor('Opening connection to %s on port %s' % (self.rhost, self.rport)) as h:
            for res in socket.getaddrinfo(self.rhost, self.rport, fam, typ, 0, socket.AI_PASSIVE):
                self.family, self.type, self.proto, _canonname, sockaddr = res

                if self.type not in [socket.SOCK_STREAM, socket.SOCK_DGRAM]:
                    continue

                h.status("Trying %s", sockaddr[0])

                sock = socket.socket(self.family, self.type, self.proto)

                if timeout is not None and timeout <= 0:

END STEP

    def recv(self, numb = None, timeout = default):
        r"""recv(numb = 4096, timeout = default) -> bytes

        Receives up to `numb` bytes of data from the tube, and returns
        as soon as any quantity of data is available.

        If the request is not satisfied before ``timeout`` seconds pass,
        all data is buffered and an empty string (``''``) is returned.

        Raises:
            exceptions.EOFError: The connection is closed

        Returns:
            A bytes object containing bytes received from the socket,
            or ``''`` if a timeout occurred while waiting.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> # Fake a data source
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda n: b'Hello, world'
            >>> t.recv() == b'Hello, world'
            True
            >>> t.unrecv(b'Woohoo')
            >>> t.recv() == b'Woohoo'
            True
            >>> with context.local(log_level='debug'):
            ...    _ = t.recv()
            [...] Received 0xc bytes:
                b'Hello, world'
        """
        numb = self.buffer.get_fill_size(numb)
        return self._recv(numb, timeout) or b''

    def unrecv(self, data):
        """unrecv(data)

        Puts the specified data back at the beginning of the receive
        buffer.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda n: b'hello'
            >>> t.recv()
            b'hello'
            >>> t.recv()
            b'hello'
            >>> t.unrecv(b'world')
            >>> t.recv()
            b'world'
            >>> t.recv()
            b'hello'
        """
        data = packing._need_bytes(data)
        self.buffer.unget(data)

    def _fillbuffer(self, timeout = default):
        """_fillbuffer(timeout = default)

        Fills the internal buffer from the pipe, by calling
        :meth:`recv_raw` exactly once.

        Returns:

            The bytes of data received, or ``''`` if no data was received.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda *a: b'abc'
            >>> len(t.buffer)
            0
            >>> t._fillbuffer()
            b'abc'
            >>> len(t.buffer)
            3
        """
        data = b''

        with self.local(timeout):
            data = self.recv_raw(self.buffer.get_fill_size())

        if data and self.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
            self.debug('Received %#x bytes:' % len(data))
            self.maybe_hexdump(data, level=logging.DEBUG)
        if data:
            self.buffer.add(data)

        return data


    def _recv(self, numb = None, timeout = default):
        """_recv(numb = 4096, timeout = default) -> str

        Receives one chunk of from the internal buffer or from the OS if the
        buffer is empty.
        """
        numb = self.buffer.get_fill_size(numb)

        # No buffered data, could not put anything in the buffer
        # before timeout.
        if not self.buffer and not self._fillbuffer(timeout):
            return b''

        return self.buffer.get(numb)

    def recvpred(self, pred, timeout = default):
        """recvpred(pred, timeout = default) -> bytes

    def recvall(self, timeout=Timeout.forever):
        """recvall(timeout=Timeout.forever) -> bytes

        Receives data until EOF is reached and closes the tube.
        """

        with self.waitfor('Receiving all data') as h:
            l = len(self.buffer)
            with self.local(timeout):
                try:
                    while True:
                        l = misc.size(len(self.buffer))
                        h.status(l)
                        if not self._fillbuffer():
                            break
                except EOFError:
                    pass
            h.success("Done (%s)" % l)
        self.close()

        return self.buffer.get()

    def send(self, data):
        """send(data)

        Sends data.

        If log level ``DEBUG`` is enabled, also prints out the data
        received.

        If it is not possible to send anymore because of a closed
        connection, it raises ``exceptions.EOFError``

        Examples:

            >>> def p(x): print(repr(x))
            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.send_raw = p
            >>> t.send(b'hello')
            b'hello'
        """

        data = packing._need_bytes(data)

        if self.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
            self.debug('Sent %#x bytes:' % len(data))
            self.maybe_hexdump(data, level=logging.DEBUG)

        self.send_raw(data)

    def sendline(self, line=b''):
        r"""sendline(data)

        Shorthand for ``t.send(data + t.newline)``.

        Examples:

            >>> def p(x): print(repr(x))
            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.send_raw = p
            >>> t.sendline(b'hello')
            b'hello\n'
            >>> t.newline = b'\r\n'
            >>> t.sendline(b'hello')
            b'hello\r\n'
        """

        line = packing._need_bytes(line)

        self.send(line + self.newline)

    def sendlines(self, lines=[]):
        for line in lines:
            line = packing._need_bytes(line)
            self.sendline(line)

    def sendafter(self, delim, data, timeout = default):
        """sendafter(delim, data, timeout = default) -> str

        A combination of ``recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)`` and ``send(data)``.
        """

        data = packing._need_bytes(data)
        res = self.recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)
        self.send(data)
        return res

    def sendlineafter(self, delim, data, timeout = default):
        """sendlineafter(delim, data, timeout = default) -> str

        A combination of ``recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)`` and ``sendline(data)``."""

        data = packing._need_bytes(data)
        res = self.recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)
        self.sendline(data)
        return res

    def sendthen(self, delim, data, timeout = default):
        """sendthen(delim, data, timeout = default) -> str

        A combination of ``send(data)`` and ``recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)``."""

        data = packing._need_bytes(data)
        self.send(data)
        return self.recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)

    def sendlinethen(self, delim, data, timeout = default):
        """sendlinethen(delim, data, timeout = default) -> str

        A combination of ``sendline(data)`` and ``recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)``."""

        data = packing._need_bytes(data)
        self.sendline(data)
        return self.recvuntil(delim, timeout=timeout)

    def interactive(self, prompt = term.text.bold_red('$') + ' '):
        """interactive(prompt = pwnlib.term.text.bold_red('$') + ' ')

        Does simultaneous reading and writing to the tube. In principle this just
        connects the tube to standard in and standard out, but in practice this
        is much more usable, since we are using :mod:`pwnlib.term` to print a
        floating prompt.

        Thus it only works while in :data:`pwnlib.term.term_mode`.
        """

        self.info('Switching to interactive mode')

        go = threading.Event()
        def recv_thread():
            while not go.is_set():
                try:
                    cur = self.recv(timeout = 0.05)
                    cur = cur.replace(self.newline, b'\n')
                    if cur:
                        stdout = sys.stdout
                        if not term.term_mode:
                            stdout = getattr(stdout, 'buffer', stdout)
                        stdout.write(cur)
                        stdout.flush()
                except EOFError:
                    self.info('Got EOF while reading in interactive')
                    break

        t = context.Thread(target = recv_thread)
        t.daemon = True
        t.start()

        from pwnlib.args import term_mode

    def can_recv(self, timeout = 0):
        """can_recv(timeout = 0) -> bool

        Returns True, if there is data available within `timeout` seconds.

        Examples:

            >>> import time
            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.can_recv_raw = lambda *a: False
            >>> t.can_recv()
            False
            >>> _=t.unrecv(b'data')
            >>> t.can_recv()
            True
            >>> _=t.recv()
            >>> t.can_recv()
            False
        """

        return bool(self.buffer or self.can_recv_raw(timeout))

    def settimeout(self, timeout):
        """settimeout(timeout)

        Set the timeout for receiving operations. If the string "default"
        is given, then :data:`context.timeout` will be used. If None is given,
        then there will be no timeout.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.settimeout_raw = lambda t: None
            >>> t.settimeout(3)
            >>> t.timeout == 3
            True
        """

        self.timeout = timeout


    shutdown_directions = {
        'in':    'recv',
        'read':  'recv',
        'recv':  'recv',
        'out':   'send',
        'write': 'send',
        'send':  'send',
    }

    connected_directions = shutdown_directions.copy()
    connected_directions['any'] = 'any'

    def shutdown(self, direction = "send"):
        """shutdown(direction = "send")

        Closes the tube for futher reading or writing depending on `direction`.

        Arguments:
          direction(str): Which direction to close; "in", "read" or "recv"
            closes the tube in the ingoing direction, "out", "write" or "send"
            closes it in the outgoing direction.

        Returns:
          :const:`None`

        Examples:

            >>> def p(x): print(x)
            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.shutdown_raw = p
            >>> _=list(map(t.shutdown, ('in', 'read', 'recv', 'out', 'write', 'send')))
            recv
            recv
            recv
            send
            send
            send
            >>> t.shutdown('bad_value')
            Traceback (most recent call last):
            ...
            KeyError: "direction must be in ['in', 'out', 'read', 'recv', 'send', 'write']"
        """
        try:
            direction = self.shutdown_directions[direction]
        except KeyError:
            raise KeyError('direction must be in %r' % sorted(self.shutdown_directions))
        else:
            self.shutdown_raw(self.shutdown_directions[direction])

    def connected(self, direction = 'any'):
        """connected(direction = 'any') -> bool

        Returns True if the tube is connected in the specified direction.

        Arguments:
          direction(str): Can be the string 'any', 'in', 'read', 'recv',
                          'out', 'write', 'send'.

        Doctest:

            >>> def p(x): print(x)
            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.connected_raw = p
            >>> _=list(map(t.connected, ('any', 'in', 'read', 'recv', 'out', 'write', 'send')))
            any
            recv
            recv
            recv
            send
            send
            send
            >>> t.connected('bad_value')
            Traceback (most recent call last):
            ...
            KeyError: "direction must be in ['any', 'in', 'out', 'read', 'recv', 'send', 'write']"
        """
        try:
            direction = self.connected_directions[direction]
        except KeyError:
            raise KeyError('direction must be in %r' % sorted(self.connected_directions))
        else:
            return self.connected_raw(direction)

    def __enter__(self):
        """Permit use of 'with' to control scoping and closing sessions.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> def p(x): print(x)
            >>> t.close = lambda: p("Closed!")
            >>> with t: pass
            Closed!
        """
        return self

    def __exit__(self, type, value, traceback):
        """Handles closing for 'with' statement

        See :meth:`__enter__`
        """
        self.close()

    # The minimal interface to be implemented by a child
    @abc.abstractmethod
    def recv_raw(self, numb):
        """recv_raw(numb) -> str

        Should not be called directly. Receives data without using the buffer
        on the object.

        Unless there is a timeout or closed connection, this should always
        return data. In case of a timeout, it should return None, in case
        of a closed connection it should raise an ``exceptions.EOFError``.
        """

        raise EOFError('Not implemented')

    @abc.abstractmethod
    def send_raw(self, data):
        """send_raw(data)

        Should not be called directly. Sends data to the tube.

        Should return ``exceptions.EOFError``, if it is unable to send any
        more, because of a closed tube.
        """

        raise EOFError('Not implemented')

    def settimeout_raw(self, timeout):
        """settimeout_raw(timeout)

        Should not be called directly. Sets the timeout for
        the tube.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError()

    def timeout_change(self):
        """
        Should not be called directly. Informs the raw layer of the tube that the timeout has changed.


        Inherited from :class:`Timeout`.
        """
        try:
            self.settimeout_raw(self.timeout)
        except NotImplementedError:
            pass

    def can_recv_raw(self, timeout):
        """can_recv_raw(timeout) -> bool

        Should not be called directly. Returns True, if
        there is data available within the timeout, but
        ignores the buffer on the object.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError()

    def connected_raw(self, direction):
        """connected(direction = 'any') -> bool

        Should not be called directly.  Returns True iff the
        tube is connected in the given direction.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError()

    def close(self):
        """close()

        Closes the tube.
        """
        pass
        # Ideally we could:
        # raise NotImplementedError()
        # But this causes issues with the unit tests.

    def fileno(self):
        """fileno() -> int

        Returns the file number used for reading.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError()

    def shutdown_raw(self, direction):
        """shutdown_raw(direction)

        Should not be called directly.  Closes the tube for further reading or
        writing.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError()

    # Dynamic functions

    def make_wrapper(func):
        def wrapperb(self, *a, **kw):
            return bytearray(func(self, *a, **kw))
        def wrapperS(self, *a, **kw):
            return packing._decode(func(self, *a, **kw))
        wrapperb.__doc__ = 'Same as :meth:`{func.__name__}`, but returns a bytearray'.format(func=func)
        wrapperb.__name__ = func.__name__ + 'b'
        wrapperS.__doc__ = 'Same as :meth:`{func.__name__}`, but returns a str, ' \
                           'decoding the result using `context.encoding`. ' \
                           '(note that the binary versions are way faster)'.format(func=func)
        wrapperS.__name__ = func.__name__ + 'S'
        return wrapperb, wrapperS

    for func in [recv,
                 recvn,
                 recvall,
                 recvrepeat,
                 recvuntil,
                 recvpred,
                 recvregex,
                 recvline,
                 recvline_contains,
                 recvline_startswith,
                 recvline_endswith,
                 recvline_regex]:
        for wrapper in make_wrapper(func):
            locals()[wrapper.__name__] = wrapper

    def make_wrapper(func, alias):
        def wrapper(self, *a, **kw):
            return func(self, *a, **kw)
        wrapper.__doc__ = 'Alias for :meth:`{func.__name__}`'.format(func=func)
        wrapper.__name__ = alias
        return wrapper

    for _name in list(locals()):
        if 'recv' in _name:
            _name2 = _name.replace('recv', 'read')
        elif 'send' in _name:
            _name2 = _name.replace('send', 'write')
        else:
            continue
        locals()[_name2] = make_wrapper(locals()[_name], _name2)

    # Clean up the scope
    del wrapper, func, make_wrapper, _name, _name2

END STEP

    62	class Timeout(object):
    63	    """
    64	    Implements a basic class which has a timeout, and support for
    65	    scoped timeout countdowns.
    66	
    67	    Valid timeout values are:
    68	
    69	    - ``Timeout.default`` use the global default value (``context.default``)
    70	    - ``Timeout.forever`` or :const:`None` never time out
    71	    - Any positive float, indicates timeouts in seconds
    72	
    73	    Example:
    74	
    75	        >>> context.timeout = 30
    76	        >>> t = Timeout()
    77	        >>> t.timeout == 30
    78	        True
    79	        >>> t = Timeout(5)
    80	        >>> t.timeout == 5
    81	        True
    82	        >>> i = 0
    83	        >>> with t.countdown():
    84	        ...     print(4 <= t.timeout and t.timeout <= 5)
    85	        ...
    86	        True
    87	        >>> with t.countdown(0.5): # doctest: +ELLIPSIS
    88	        ...     while t.timeout:
    89	        ...         print(round(t.timeout,1))
    90	        ...         time.sleep(0.1)
    91	        0.5
    92	        0.4
    93	        0.3
    94	        0.2
    95	        0.1
    96	        >>> print(t.timeout)
    97	        5.0
    98	        >>> with t.local(0.5):# doctest: +ELLIPSIS
    99	        ...     for i in range(5):
   100	        ...         print(round(t.timeout,1))
   101	        ...         time.sleep(0.1)
   102	        0.5
   103	        0.5
   104	        0.5
   105	        0.5
   106	        ...
   107	        >>> print(t.timeout)
   108	        5.0
   109	    """
   110	
   111	
   112	    #: Value indicating that the timeout should not be changed
   113	    default = TimeoutDefault()
   114	
   115	    #: Value indicating that a timeout should not ever occur
   116	    forever = None
   117	
   118	    #: Maximum value for a timeout.  Used to get around platform issues
   119	    #: with very large timeouts.
   120	    #:
   121	    #: OSX does not permit setting socket timeouts to 2**22.
   122	    #: Assume that if we receive a timeout of 2**21 or greater,
   123	    #: that the value is effectively infinite.
   124	    maximum = maximum
   125	
   126	    def __init__(self, timeout=default):
   127	        self._stop    = 0
   128	        self.timeout = self._get_timeout_seconds(timeout)
   129	
   130	    @property
   131	    def timeout(self):
   132	        """
   133	        Timeout for obj operations.  By default, uses ``context.timeout``.
   134	        """
   135	        timeout = self._timeout
   136	        stop    = self._stop
   137	
   138	        if not stop:
   139	            return timeout
   140	
   141	        return max(stop-time.time(), 0)
   142	
   143	    @timeout.setter
   144	    def timeout(self, value):
   145	        assert not self._stop
   146	        self._timeout = self._get_timeout_seconds(value)
   147	        self.timeout_change()
   148	
   149	    def _get_timeout_seconds(self, value):
   150	        if value is self.default:
   151	            value = pwnlib.context.context.timeout
   152	
   153	        elif value is self.forever:
   154	            value = self.maximum
   155	
   156	        else:
   157	            value = float(value)
   158	
   159	            if value < 0:
   160	                raise AttributeError("timeout: Timeout cannot be negative")
   161	
   162	            if value > self.maximum:
   163	                value = self.maximum
   164	        return value
   165	
   166	    def countdown_active(self):
   167	        return (self._stop == 0) or (self._stop > time.time())
   168	
   169	    def timeout_change(self):
   170	        """
   171	        Callback for subclasses to hook a timeout change.
   172	        """
   173	        pass
   174	
   175	    def countdown(self, timeout = default):
   176	        """
   177	        Scoped timeout setter.  Sets the timeout within the scope,
   178	        and restores it when leaving the scope.
   179	
   180	        When accessing :attr:`timeout` within the scope, it will be
   181	        calculated against the time when the scope was entered, in a
   182	        countdown fashion.
   183	
   184	        If :const:`None` is specified for ``timeout``, then the current
   185	        timeout is used is made.  This allows :const:`None` to be specified
   186	        as a default argument with less complexity.
   187	        """
   188	        # Don't count down from infinity
   189	        if timeout is self.maximum:
   190	            return _DummyContext
   191	
   192	        if timeout is self.default and self.timeout is self.maximum:
   193	            return _DummyContext
   194	
   195	        if timeout is self.default:
   196	            timeout = self._timeout
   197	
   198	        return _countdown_handler(self, timeout)
   199	
   200	    def local(self, timeout):
   201	        """
   202	        Scoped timeout setter.  Sets the timeout within the scope,
   203	        and restores it when leaving the scope.
   204	        """
   205	        if timeout is self.default or timeout == self.timeout:
   206	            return _DummyContext
   207	
   208	        return _local_handler(self, timeout)

END STEP

    10	class sock(tube):
    11	    """Base type used for :class:`.tubes.remote` and :class:`.tubes.listen` classes"""
    12	
    13	    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
    14	        super(sock, self).__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    15	        self.closed = {"recv": False, "send": False}
    16	
    17	    # Overwritten for better usability
    18	    def recvall(self, timeout = tube.forever):
    19	        """recvall() -> str
    20	
    21	        Receives data until the socket is closed.
    22	        """
    23	
    24	        if getattr(self, 'type', None) == socket.SOCK_DGRAM:
    25	            self.error("UDP sockets do not support recvall")
    26	        else:
    27	            return super(sock, self).recvall(timeout)
    28	
    29	    def recv_raw(self, numb, *a):
    30	        if self.closed["recv"]:
    31	            raise EOFError
    32	
    33	        while True:
    34	            try:
    35	                data = self.sock.recv(numb, *a)
    36	                break
    37	            except socket.timeout:
    38	                return None
    39	            except IOError as e:
    40	                if e.errno in (errno.EAGAIN, errno.ETIMEDOUT) or 'timed out' in e.strerror:
    41	                    return None
    42	                elif e.errno in (errno.ECONNREFUSED, errno.ECONNRESET):
    43	                    self.shutdown("recv")
    44	                    raise EOFError
    45	                elif e.errno == errno.EINTR:
    46	                    continue
    47	                else:
    48	                    raise
    49	
    50	        if not data:
    51	            self.shutdown("recv")
    52	            raise EOFError
    53	
    54	        return data
    55	
    56	    def send_raw(self, data):
    57	        if self.closed["send"]:
    58	            raise EOFError
    59	
    60	        try:
    61	            self.sock.sendall(data)
    62	        except IOError as e:
    63	            eof_numbers = (errno.EPIPE, errno.ECONNRESET, errno.ECONNREFUSED)
    64	            if e.errno in eof_numbers or 'Socket is closed' in e.args:
    65	                self.shutdown("send")
    66	                raise EOFError
    67	            else:
    68	                raise
    69	
    70	    def settimeout_raw(self, timeout):
    71	        sock = getattr(self, 'sock', None)
    72	        if sock:
    73	            sock.settimeout(timeout)
    74	
    75	    def can_recv_raw(self, timeout):
    76	        """
    77	        Tests:
    78	
    79	            >>> l = listen()
    80	            >>> r = remote('localhost', l.lport)
    81	            >>> r.can_recv_raw(timeout=0)
    82	            False
    83	            >>> l.send(b'a')
    84	            >>> r.can_recv_raw(timeout=1)
    85	            True
    86	            >>> r.recv()
    87	            b'a'
    88	            >>> r.can_recv_raw(timeout=0)
    89	            False
    90	            >>> l.close()
    91	            >>> r.can_recv_raw(timeout=1)
    92	            False
    93	            >>> r.closed['recv']
    94	            True
    95	        """
    96	        if not self.sock or self.closed["recv"]:
    97	            return False
    98	
    99	        # select() will tell us data is available at EOF
   100	        can_recv = select.select([self.sock], [], [], timeout) == ([self.sock], [], [])
   101	
   102	        if not can_recv:
   103	            return False
   104	
   105	        # Ensure there's actually data, not just EOF
   106	        try:
   107	            self.recv_raw(1, socket.MSG_PEEK)
   108	        except EOFError:
   109	            return False
   110	
   111	        return True
   112	
   113	    def connected_raw(self, direction):
   114	        """
   115	        Tests:
   116	
   117	            >>> l = listen()
   118	            >>> r = remote('localhost', l.lport)
   119	            >>> r.connected()
   120	            True
   121	            >>> l.close()
   122	            >>> time.sleep(0.1) # Avoid race condition
   123	            >>> r.connected()
   124	            False
   125	        """
   126	        # If there's no socket, it's definitely closed
   127	        if not self.sock:
   128	            return False
   129	
   130	        # If we have noticed a connection close in a given direction before,
   131	        # return fast.
   132	        if self.closed.get(direction, False):
   133	            return False
   134	
   135	        # If a connection is closed in all manners, return fast
   136	        if all(self.closed.values()):
   137	            return False
   138	
   139	        # Use poll() to determine the connection state
   140	        want = {
   141	            'recv': select.POLLIN,
   142	            'send': select.POLLOUT,
   143	            'any':  select.POLLIN | select.POLLOUT,
   144	        }[direction]
   145	
   146	        poll = select.poll()
   147	        poll.register(self, want | select.POLLHUP | select.POLLERR)
   148	
   149	        for fd, event in poll.poll(0):
   150	            if event & select.POLLHUP:
   151	                self.close()
   152	                return False
   153	            if event & select.POLLIN:
   154	                return True
   155	            if event & select.POLLOUT:
   156	                return True
   157	
   158	        return True
   159	
   160	    def close(self):
   161	        sock = getattr(self, 'sock', None)
   162	        if not sock:
   163	            return
   164	
   165	        # Mark as closed in both directions
   166	        self.closed['send'] = True
   167	        self.closed['recv'] = True
   168	
   169	        sock.close()
   170	        self.sock = None
   171	        self._close_msg()
   172	
   173	    def _close_msg(self):
   174	        self.info('Closed connection to %s port %s', self.rhost, self.rport)
   175	
   176	    def fileno(self):
   177	        if not self.sock:
   178	            self.error("A closed socket does not have a file number")
   179	
   180	        return self.sock.fileno()
   181	
   182	    def shutdown_raw(self, direction):
   183	        if self.closed[direction]:
   184	            return
   185	
   186	        self.closed[direction] = True
   187	
   188	        if direction == "send":
   189	            try:
   190	                self.sock.shutdown(socket.SHUT_WR)
   191	            except IOError as e:
   192	                if e.errno == errno.ENOTCONN:
   193	                    pass
   194	                else:
   195	                    raise
   196	
   197	        if direction == "recv":
   198	            try:
   199	                self.sock.shutdown(socket.SHUT_RD)
   200	            except IOError as e:
   201	                if e.errno == errno.ENOTCONN:
   202	                    pass
   203	                else:
   204	                    raise
   205	
   206	        if False not in self.closed.values():
   207	            self.close()
   208	
   209	    @classmethod
   210	    def _get_family(cls, fam):
   211	        if isinstance(fam, int):
   212	            pass
   213	        elif fam == 'any':
   214	            fam = socket.AF_UNSPEC
   215	        elif fam.lower() in ['ipv4', 'ip4', 'v4', '4']:
   216	            fam = socket.AF_INET
   217	        elif fam.lower() in ['ipv6', 'ip6', 'v6', '6']:
   218	            fam = socket.AF_INET6
   219	        else:
   220	            self.error("%s(): socket family %r is not supported",

returncode=0
__all__ = ['PwnlibException']
import sys
import traceback


class PwnlibException(Exception):
    '''Exception thrown by :func:`pwnlib.log.error`.

    Pwnlib functions that encounters unrecoverable errors should call the
    :func:`pwnlib.log.error` function instead of throwing this exception directly.'''
    def __init__(self, msg, reason = None, exit_code = None):
        '''bar'''
        Exception.__init__(self, msg)
        self.reason = reason
        self.exit_code = exit_code
        self.message = msg

    def __repr__(self):
        s = 'PwnlibException: %s' % self.message
        if self.reason:
            s += '\nReason:\n'
            s += ''.join(traceback.format_exception(*self.reason))
        elif sys.exc_info()[0] not in [None, KeyboardInterrupt]:
            s += '\n'
            s += ''.join(traceback.format_exc())
        return s

returncode=0
    def _recv(self, numb = None, timeout = default):
        """_recv(numb = 4096, timeout = default) -> str

        Receives one chunk of from the internal buffer or from the OS if the
        buffer is empty.
        """
        numb = self.buffer.get_fill_size(numb)

        # No buffered data, could not put anything in the buffer
        # before timeout.
        if not self.buffer and not self._fillbuffer(timeout):
            return b''

        return self.buffer.get(numb)

    def recvpred(self, pred, timeout = default):
        """recvpred(pred, timeout = default) -> bytes

        Receives one byte at a time from the tube, until ``pred(all_bytes)``
        evaluates to True.

        If the request is not satisfied before ``timeout`` seconds pass,
        all data is buffered and an empty string (``''``) is returned.

        Arguments:
            pred(callable): Function to call, with the currently-accumulated data.
            timeout(int): Timeout for the operation

        Raises:
            exceptions.EOFError: The connection is closed

        Returns:
            A bytes object containing bytes received from the socket,
            or ``''`` if a timeout occurred while waiting.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda n: b'abbbaccc'
            >>> pred = lambda p: p.count(b'a') == 2
            >>> t.recvpred(pred)
            b'abbba'
            >>> pred = lambda p: p.count(b'd') > 0
            >>> t.recvpred(pred, timeout=0.05)
            b''
        """

        data = b''

        with self.countdown(timeout):
            while not pred(data):
                if not self.countdown_active():
                    self.unrecv(data)
                    return b''

                try:
                    res = self.recv(1, timeout=timeout)
                except Exception:
                    self.unrecv(data)
                    return b''

                if res:
                    data += res
                else:
                    self.unrecv(data)
                    return b''

        return data

    def recvn(self, numb, timeout = default):
        """recvn(numb, timeout = default) -> bytes

        Receives exactly `n` bytes.

        If the request is not satisfied before ``timeout`` seconds pass,
        all data is buffered and an empty string (``''``) is returned.

        Raises:
            exceptions.EOFError: The connection closed before the request could be satisfied

        Returns:
            A string containing bytes received from the socket,
            or ``''`` if a timeout occurred while waiting.

        Examples:

            >>> t = tube()
            >>> data = b'hello world'
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda *a: data
            >>> t.recvn(len(data)) == data
            True
            >>> t.recvn(len(data)+1) == data + data[:1]
            True
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda *a: None
            >>> # The remaining data is buffered
            >>> t.recv() == data[1:]
            True
            >>> t.recv_raw = lambda *a: time.sleep(0.01) or b'a'
            >>> t.recvn(10, timeout=0.05)
            b''
            >>> t.recvn(10, timeout=0.06)
            b'aaaaaa...'
        """
        # Keep track of how much data has been received
        # It will be pasted together at the end if a
        # timeout does not occur, or put into the tube buffer.
        with self.countdown(timeout):
            while self.countdown_active() and len(self.buffer) < numb and self._fillbuffer(self.timeout):
                pass

        if len(self.buffer) < numb:
            return b''

        return self.buffer.get(numb)

    def recvuntil(self, delims, drop=False, timeout=default):
        """recvuntil(delims, drop=False, timeout=default) -> bytes

        Receive data until one of `delims` is encountered.

        If the request is not satisfied before ``timeout`` seconds pass,
        all data is buffered and an empty string (``''``) is returned.

END STEP

class listen(sock):
    r"""Creates an TCP or UDP-socket to receive data on. It supports
    both IPv4 and IPv6. You need to call :meth:`wait_for_connection`
    before using the listen socket.

    The returned object supports all the methods from
    :class:`pwnlib.tubes.sock` and :class:`pwnlib.tubes.tube`.

    Arguments:
        port(int): The port to connect to.
            Defaults to a port auto-selected by the operating system.
        bindaddr(str): The address to bind to.
            Defaults to ``0.0.0.0`` / `::`.
        fam: The string "any", "ipv4" or "ipv6" or an integer to pass to :func:`socket.getaddrinfo`.
        typ: The string "tcp" or "udp" or an integer to pass to :func:`socket.getaddrinfo`.

    Examples:

        >>> l = listen(1234)
        >>> r = remote('localhost', l.lport)
        >>> _ = l.wait_for_connection()
        >>> l.sendline(b'Hello')
        >>> r.recvline()
        b'Hello\n'

        .. doctest::
            :options: +POSIX +TODO
            
            >>> # It works with ipv4 by default
            >>> l = listen()
            >>> l.spawn_process('/bin/sh')
            >>> r = remote('127.0.0.1', l.lport)
            >>> r.sendline(b'echo Goodbye')
            >>> r.recvline()
            b'Goodbye\n'

        >>> # and it works with ipv6 by defaut, too!
        >>> l = listen()
        >>> r = remote('::1', l.lport)
        >>> _ = l.wait_for_connection()
        >>> r.sendline(b'Bye-bye')
        >>> l.recvline()
        b'Bye-bye\n'
    """

    lport = 0

    #: Local host
    lhost = None

    #: Socket type (e.g. socket.SOCK_STREAM)
    type = None

    #: Socket family
    family = None

    #: Socket protocol
    protocol = None

    #: Canonical name of the listening interface
    canonname = None

    #: Sockaddr structure that is being listened on
    sockaddr = None

    _accepter = None

    def __init__(self, port=0, bindaddr='::',
                 fam='any', typ='tcp', *args, **kwargs):

END STEP

    @classmethod
    def fromsocket(cls, socket):
        """
        Helper method to wrap a standard python socket.socket with the
        tube APIs.

        Arguments:
            socket: Instance of socket.socket

        Returns:
            Instance of pwnlib.tubes.remote.remote.

        return remote(host, port, fam=s.family, typ=s.type, sock=s)

cd /app && cat > pwnlib/tubes/mux.py <<'PYEOF'
"""
Multiplex multiple logical, bidirectional channels over a single underlying tube.

This module provides :class:`TubeMultiplexer`, which frames data sent over a
single underlying :class:`pwnlib.tubes.tube.tube` into multiple independent
logical channels, and :class:`MuxChannel`, the tube-like object returned for
each logical channel.
"""

import collections
import logging
import struct
import threading
import time

from pwnlib.log import getLogger
from pwnlib.timeout import Timeout
from pwnlib.tubes.tube import tube

log = getLogger(__name__)

# Message types used in the framing protocol
_MSG_OPEN   = 1   # Open a new channel
_MSG_DATA   = 2   # Data for an open channel
_MSG_CLOSE  = 3   # Fully close a channel
_MSG_ACK    = 4   # Acknowledge a channel open
_MSG_NACK   = 5   # Reject a channel open
_MSG_PAUSE  = 6   # Ask the peer to pause sending on this channel
_MSG_RESUME = 7   # Ask the peer to resume sending on this channel
_MSG_FIN    = 8   # Half-close: local send direction is finished

_HDR_FMT = "!II"
_HDRLEN = struct.calcsize(_HDR_FMT)

_MAX_CHANNEL_ID = 65535


class TubeMultiplexer(object):
    """Multiplex multiple logical channels over one underlying tube.

    Arguments:
        underlying(tube): The underlying tube to multiplex over.
        max_channels(int): Maximum number of simultaneously open channels.
            Must be between 1 and 65535.
        high_water_mark(int): Number of queued bytes for a channel which
            causes the remote sender to be paused.
        low_water_mark(int): Number of queued bytes for a channel which
            causes the remote sender to be resumed.

    Examples:

        >>> import socket
        >>> s1, s2 = socket.socketpair()
        >>> from pwnlib.tubes.remote import remote
        >>> t1 = remote.fromsocket(s1)
        >>> t2 = remote.fromsocket(s2)
        >>> m1 = t1.mux()
        >>> m2 = t2.mux()
        >>> c1 = m1.open_channel(1)
        >>> c2 = m2.accept_channel(timeout=1)
        >>> c1.send(b'hello')
        >>> c2.recv() == b'hello'
        True
    """

    def __init__(self, underlying, max_channels=256,
                 high_water_mark=1048576, low_water_mark=262144):
        if not isinstance(underlying, tube):
            raise TypeError("underlying must be an instance of pwnlib.tubes.tube.tube")
        if not isinstance(max_channels, int) or isinstance(max_channels, bool) \
                or max_channels < 1 or max_channels > _MAX_CHANNEL_ID:
            raise ValueError("max_channels must be an integer in [1, 65535]")
        if low_water_mark > high_water_mark:
            raise ValueError("low_water_mark cannot exceed high_water_mark")

        self._underlying = underlying
        self._max_channels = max_channels
        self._high_water_mark = high_water_mark
        self._low_water_mark = low_water_mark

        #: Dictionary mapping open channel IDs to their :class:`MuxChannel`.
        self.channels = {}

        self._channels_lock = threading.RLock()
        self._write_lock = threading.Lock()
        self._closed = False
        self._closed_cond = threading.Condition()

        # Queue of channels accepted from the remote side, for accept_channel()
        self._accept_cond = threading.Condition()
        self._accept_queue = collections.deque()

        self._next_channel_id = 1

        # Start the reader thread which consumes frames from the underlying tube.
        self._reader_thread = threading.Thread(
            target=self._reader_loop, daemon=True,
            name="pwntools-tube-mux-reader")
        self._reader_thread.start()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public properties
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def max_channels(self):
        return self._max_channels

    @property
    def high_water_mark(self):
        return self._high_water_mark

    @property
    def low_water_mark(self):
        return self._low_water_mark

    @property
    def closed(self):
        return self._closed

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Channel management
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def open_channel(self, channel_id=None, timeout=None):
        """Open a new logical channel and wait for remote acknowledgement.

        Arguments:
            channel_id(int): Explicit channel ID, or None to auto-allocate.
            timeout(float): Maximum time in seconds to wait for the remote
                acknowledgement.  ``None`` waits forever.

        Returns:
            The newly created :class:`MuxChannel`.

        Raises:
            TypeError: If ``channel_id`` is not an integer.
            ValueError: If ``channel_id`` is out of range, already in use, or
                exceeds the multiplexer capacity.
            TimeoutError: If the remote does not acknowledge in time.
            EOFError: If the multiplexer is closed.
        """
        if self._closed:
            raise EOFError("multiplexer is closed")

        if channel_id is None:
            channel_id = self._allocate_channel_id()
        else:
            if not isinstance(channel_id, int) or isinstance(channel_id, bool):
                raise TypeError("channel_id must be an integer")
            if channel_id < 1 or channel_id > _MAX_CHANNEL_ID:
                raise ValueError("channel_id must be in [1, 65535]")
            if channel_id > self._max_channels:
                raise ValueError("channel_id exceeds max_channels")
            with self._channels_lock:
                if channel_id in self.channels:
                    raise ValueError("channel_id already in use")
                if len(self.channels) >= self._max_channels:
                    raise ValueError("max_channels exceeded")

        channel = MuxChannel(self, channel_id)

        with self._channels_lock:
            if self._closed:
                raise EOFError("multiplexer is closed")
            if channel_id in self.channels:
                raise ValueError("channel_id already in use")
            if len(self.channels) >= self._max_channels:
                raise ValueError("max_channels exceeded")
            self.channels[channel_id] = channel

        try:
            self._send_frame(channel_id, _MSG_OPEN)
        except EOFError:
            self._remove_channel(channel_id)
            raise

        if not channel._open_event.wait(timeout):
            # Clean up the failed channel and tell the remote side to forget it.
            self._remove_channel(channel_id)
            try:
                self._send_frame(channel_id, _MSG_CLOSE)
            except EOFError:
                pass
            raise TimeoutError("timed out waiting for channel acknowledgement")

        if channel._open_failed:
            self._remove_channel(channel_id)
            raise EOFError("remote refused to open channel")

        return channel

    def _allocate_channel_id(self):
        with self._channels_lock:
            if len(self.channels) >= self._max_channels:
                raise ValueError("max_channels exceeded")
            start = self._next_channel_id
            cid = start
            for _ in range(self._max_channels):
                if cid not in self.channels:
                    self._next_channel_id = cid + 1
                    if self._next_channel_id > self._max_channels:
                        self._next_channel_id = 1
                    return cid
                cid += 1
                if cid > self._max_channels:
                    cid = 1
                if cid == start:
                    break
            raise ValueError("max_channels exceeded")

    def _remove_channel(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            self.channels.pop(channel_id, None)

    def accept_channel(self, timeout=None):
        """Wait for the remote side to open a channel.

        Arguments:
            timeout(float): Maximum time in seconds to wait.  ``None`` waits
                forever.

        Returns:
            The accepted :class:`MuxChannel`, or ``None`` if the timeout
            expired before a channel was opened.

        Raises:
            EOFError: If the multiplexer is closed.
        """
        deadline = None if timeout is None else time.time() + timeout

        with self._accept_cond:
            while not self._accept_queue and not self._closed:
                if deadline is None:
                    self._accept_cond.wait()
                else:
                    remaining = deadline - time.time()
                    if remaining <= 0:
                        return None
                    self._accept_cond.wait(remaining)

            if self._closed:
                raise EOFError("multiplexer is closed")

            if self._accept_queue:
                return self._accept_queue.popleft()

            return None

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Closing
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def close(self):
        """Close the multiplexer and all channels.

        Signals EOF to every channel, closes the underlying tube, and is
        idempotent.  Threads blocked in :meth:`accept_channel` are woken up
        with :class:`EOFError`.
        """
        with self._closed_cond:
            if self._closed:
                return
            self._closed = True
            self._closed_cond.notify_all()

        with self._channels_lock:
            channels = list(self.channels.values())
            self.channels.clear()

        for channel in channels:
            channel._mux_closed()

        with self._accept_cond:
            self._accept_cond.notify_all()

        try:
            self._underlying.close()
        except Exception:
            pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Reader thread
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _reader_loop(self):
        try:
            while True:
                try:
                    header = self._underlying.recvn(_HDRLEN, timeout=Timeout.forever)
                except EOFError:
                    break
                if not header or len(header) < _HDRLEN:
                    break

                channel_id, length = struct.unpack(_HDR_FMT, header)
                payload = self._underlying.recvn(length, timeout=Timeout.forever)
                if payload is None or len(payload) < length:
                    break

                self._dispatch(channel_id, payload)
        except Exception:
            log.debug("TubeMultiplexer reader thread died", exc_info=True)
        finally:
            self._handle_underlying_eof()

    def _dispatch(self, channel_id, payload):
        if not payload:
            return

        msgtype = payload[0]
        body = payload[1:]

        if msgtype == _MSG_OPEN:
            self._handle_open(channel_id)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_ACK:
            self._handle_ack(channel_id)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_NACK:
            self._handle_nack(channel_id)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_DATA:
            self._handle_data(channel_id, body)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_CLOSE:
            self._handle_close(channel_id)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_FIN:
            self._handle_fin(channel_id)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_PAUSE:
            self._handle_pause(channel_id)
        elif msgtype == _MSG_RESUME:
            self._handle_resume(channel_id)
        else:
            log.debug("Unknown multiplexer message type %r on channel %r",
                      msgtype, channel_id)

    def _handle_open(self, channel_id):
        if self._closed:
            return

        with self._channels_lock:
            if channel_id in self.channels:
                return
            if len(self.channels) >= self._max_channels:
                # Cannot accept another channel; reject the open request.
                try:
                    self._send_frame(channel_id, _MSG_NACK)
                except EOFError:
                    pass
                return
            channel = MuxChannel(self, channel_id)
            self.channels[channel_id] = channel

        try:
            self._send_frame(channel_id, _MSG_ACK)
        except EOFError:
            return

        with self._accept_cond:
            self._accept_queue.append(channel)
            self._accept_cond.notify_all()

    def _handle_ack(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.get(channel_id)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._open_event.set()

    def _handle_nack(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.get(channel_id)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._open_failed = True
            channel._open_event.set()

    def _handle_data(self, channel_id, data):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.get(channel_id)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._recv_data(data)

    def _handle_close(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.pop(channel_id, None)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._remote_closed()

    def _handle_fin(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.get(channel_id)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._remote_fin()

    def _handle_pause(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.get(channel_id)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._remote_pause()

    def _handle_resume(self, channel_id):
        with self._channels_lock:
            channel = self.channels.get(channel_id)
        if channel is not None:
            channel._remote_resume()

    def _handle_underlying_eof(self):
        """Propagate death of the underlying tube to all channels."""
        with self._closed_cond:
            if self._closed:
                return
            self._closed = True
            self._closed_cond.notify_all()

        with self._channels_lock:
            channels = list(self.channels.values())
            self.channels.clear()

        for channel in channels:
            channel._mux_closed()

        with self._accept_cond:
            self._accept_cond.notify_all()

        try:
            self._underlying.close()
        except Exception:
            pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Frame I/O
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _make_frame(self, channel_id, msgtype, body=b''):
        payload = bytes((msgtype,)) + body
        return struct.pack(_HDR_FMT, channel_id, len(payload)) + payload

    def _send_frame(self, channel_id, msgtype, body=b''):
        if self._closed:
            raise EOFError("multiplexer is closed")

        frame = self._make_frame(channel_id, msgtype, body)
        try:
            with self._write_lock:
                self._underlying.send(frame)
        except EOFError:
            self._handle_underlying_eof()
            raise
        except Exception:
            self._handle_underlying_eof()
            raise

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Flow control
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _maybe_pause(self, channel):
        send = False
        with channel._recv_cond:
            if not channel._flow_paused and \
                    channel._recv_size > self._high_water_mark:
                channel._flow_paused = True
                send = True
        if send:
            try:
                self._send_frame(channel.channel_id, _MSG_PAUSE)
            except EOFError:
                pass

    def _maybe_resume(self, channel):
        send = False
        with channel._recv_cond:
            if channel._flow_paused and \
                    channel._recv_size <= self._low_water_mark:
                channel._flow_paused = False
                send = True
        if send:
            try:
                self._send_frame(channel.channel_id, _MSG_RESUME)
            except EOFError:
                pass

    def _send_channel_data(self, channel, data):
        timeout = channel.timeout
        if timeout >= channel.maximum:
            deadline = None
        else:
            deadline = time.time() + timeout

        while True:
            with channel._send_cond:
                if self._closed or channel._send_eof:
                    raise EOFError("channel is closed")

                if not channel._peer_paused:
                    break

                if deadline is None:
                    channel._send_cond.wait()
                else:
                    remaining = deadline - time.time()
                    if remaining <= 0:
                        raise TimeoutError(
                            "timed out waiting for flow control on channel %r"
                            % channel.channel_id)
                    channel._send_cond.wait(remaining)

        self._send_frame(channel.channel_id, _MSG_DATA, data)

        with channel._stats_lock:
            channel._stats["bytes_sent"] += len(data)
            channel._stats["frames_sent"] += 1


class MuxChannel(tube):
    """A single logical channel inside a :class:`TubeMultiplexer`.

    Instances are tube objects, so they support the full ``recv``/``send``
    family of methods.
    """

    def __init__(self, mux, channel_id, timeout=tube.default):
        super(MuxChannel, self).__init__(timeout=timeout)
        self._mux = mux
        self._channel_id = channel_id

        # Receive path
        self._recv_queue = collections.deque()
        self._recv_size = 0
        self._recv_eof = False
        self._recv_cond = threading.Condition()

        # Send path / flow control
        self._send_eof = False
        self._send_cond = threading.Condition()
        self._peer_paused = False
        self._flow_paused = False

        # Open handshake
        self._open_event = threading.Event()
        self._open_failed = False

        # Statistics
        self._stats = {
            "bytes_sent": 0,
            "bytes_received": 0,
            "frames_sent": 0,
            "frames_received": 0,
        }
        self._stats_lock = threading.Lock()

        self.buffer.set_watermarks(high=mux.high_water_mark,
                                   low=mux.low_water_mark)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public properties
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def channel_id(self):
        """The numeric ID of this channel."""
        return self._channel_id

    @property
    def stats(self):
        """A dict with byte/frame counters for this channel."""
        with self._stats_lock:
            return dict(self._stats)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Receive path
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _recv_data(self, data):
        """Called by the multiplexer reader thread when DATA arrives."""
        with self._recv_cond:
            self._recv_queue.append(data)
            self._recv_size += len(data)
            self._recv_cond.notify_all()

        with self._stats_lock:
            self._stats["bytes_received"] += len(data)
            self._stats["frames_received"] += 1

        self._mux._maybe_pause(self)

    def _recv_data_chunk(self, timeout=tube.default):
        """Block until a chunk is available or the timeout elapses."""
        timeout = self._get_timeout_seconds(timeout)
        deadline = None if timeout >= self.maximum else time.time() + timeout

        with self._recv_cond:
            while not self._recv_queue and not self._recv_eof:
                if deadline is None:
                    self._recv_cond.wait()
                else:
                    remaining = deadline - time.time()
                    if remaining <= 0:
                        return None
                    self._recv_cond.wait(remaining)

            if self._recv_eof and not self._recv_queue:
                raise EOFError("channel is closed")

            if not self._recv_queue:
                return None

            data = self._recv_queue.popleft()
            self._recv_size -= len(data)
            return data

    def _fillbuffer(self, timeout=tube.default):
        data = self._recv_data_chunk(timeout)
        if data:
            self.buffer.add(data)
            self._mux._maybe_resume(self)
        return data

    def _recv(self, numb=None, timeout=tube.default):
        numb = self.buffer.get_fill_size(numb)

        if not self.buffer and not self._fillbuffer(timeout):
            return b""

        result = self.buffer.get(numb)
        self._mux._maybe_resume(self)
        return result

    def recv_raw(self, numb):
        data = self._recv_data_chunk(self.timeout)
        if data is None:
            return None
        return data

    def can_recv_raw(self, timeout):
        with self._recv_cond:
            if self._recv_queue:
                return True
            if self._recv_eof:
                return False
            if timeout:
                self._recv_cond.wait(timeout)
            return bool(self._recv_queue)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Send path
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def send_raw(self, data):
        self._mux._send_channel_data(self, data)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Remote control messages
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _remote_pause(self):
        with self._send_cond:
            self._peer_paused = True

    def _remote_resume(self):
        with self._send_cond:
            self._peer_paused = False
            self._send_cond.notify_all()

    def _remote_fin(self):
        with self._recv_cond:
            self._recv_eof = True
            self._recv_cond.notify_all()

    def _remote_closed(self):
        with self._recv_cond:
            self._recv_eof = True
            self._recv_cond.notify_all()
        with self._send_cond:
            self._send_eof = True
            self._send_cond.notify_all()

    def _mux_closed(self):
        with self._recv_cond:
            self._recv_eof = True
            self._recv_cond.notify_all()
        with self._send_cond:
            self._send_eof = True
            self._send_cond.notify_all()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # tube interface
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def settimeout_raw(self, timeout):
        pass

    def connected_raw(self, direction):
        if self._mux.closed:
            return False

        if direction == "recv":
            return not self._recv_eof
        if direction == "send":
            return not self._send_eof
        if direction == "any":
            return not (self._recv_eof and self._send_eof)
        return False

    def shutdown_raw(self, direction):
        if direction == "send":
            with self._send_cond:
                if self._send_eof:
                    return
                self._send_eof = True
                self._send_cond.notify_all()
            try:
                self._mux._send_frame(self._channel_id, _MSG_FIN)
            except EOFError:
                pass

        elif direction == "recv":
            with self._recv_cond:
                if self._recv_eof:
                    return
                self._recv_eof = True
                self._recv_cond.notify_all()

    def close(self):
        """Close this channel and signal EOF to the remote peer."""
        with self._recv_cond:
            recv_already = self._recv_eof
            self._recv_eof = True
            self._recv_cond.notify_all()
        with self._send_cond:
            send_already = self._send_eof
            self._send_eof = True
            self._send_cond.notify_all()

        self._mux._remove_channel(self._channel_id)

        if not (recv_already and send_already):
            try:
                self._mux._send_frame(self._channel_id, _MSG_CLOSE)
            except EOFError:
                pass

    def fileno(self):
        raise NotImplementedError("MuxChannel has no file descriptor")

"""The pwnlib is not a big truck! It's a series of tubes!

This is our library for talking to sockets, processes, ssh connections etc.
Our goal is to be able to use the same API for e.g. remote TCP servers, local
TTY-programs and programs run over over SSH.

It is organized such that the majority of the functionality is implemented
in :class:`pwnlib.tubes.tube`. The remaining classes should only implement
just enough for the class to work and possibly code pertaining only to
that specific kind of tube.
"""
from pwnlib.tubes import listen
from pwnlib.tubes import mux
from pwnlib.tubes import process
from pwnlib.tubes import remote
from pwnlib.tubes import serialtube
from pwnlib.tubes import server
from pwnlib.tubes import sock
from pwnlib.tubes import ssh
from pwnlib.tubes import tube

__all__ = ['tube', 'sock', 'remote', 'listen', 'mux', 'process', 'serialtube', 'server', 'ssh']

import socket, time, threading
from pwnlib.tubes.sock import sock
from pwnlib.timeout import Timeout
s1, s2 = socket.socketpair()
t1 = sock(timeout=None); t2 = sock(timeout=None)
t1.sock = s1; t2.sock = s2
print("t1 timeout", t1.timeout, "sock timeout", s1.gettimeout())
def rdr():
    try:
        print("reading...")
        data = t1.recvn(8, timeout=Timeout.forever)
        print("got", data)
    except Exception as e:
        print("reader exception", type(e).__name__, e)
threading.Thread(target=rdr, daemon=True).start()
time.sleep(0.3)
t2.send(b'12345678')
time.sleep(0.5)

STEP 31 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && sed -n '160,210p' pwnlib/tubes/listen.py
OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
    def wait_for_connection(self):
        """Blocks until a connection has been established."""
        self.sock
        return self

    @property
    def sock(self):
        try:
            return self.__dict__['sock']
        except KeyError:
            pass
        self._accepter.join(timeout=self.timeout)
        return self.__dict__.get('sock')

    @sock.setter
    def sock(self, s):
        self.__dict__['sock'] = s

    def close(self):
        # since `close` is scheduled to run on exit we must check that we got
        # a connection or the program will hang in the `join` call above
        if self._accepter and self._accepter.is_alive():
            return
        super(listen, self).close()

END STEP

import socket, time, threading

from pwnlib.tubes.remote import remote

r = remote('localhost', l.lport)

m2 = srv.mux(max_channels=10, high_water_mark=1024, low_water_mark=256)

c1 = m1.open_channel(1, timeout=1)
print("opened c1 id", c1.channel_id, "stats", c1.stats)
c2 = m2.accept_channel(timeout=1)
print("accepted c2 id", c2.channel_id, "stats", c2.stats)

c1.send(b'hello')
print("c2.recv:", c2.recv())
print("c1 stats:", c1.stats)

                 ("c2.send", lambda: c2.send(b'x')),
                 ("c1.send", lambda: c1.send(b'x'))]:
    try:
        fn()
        print("ERROR", name, "should EOF")
    except EOFError:
        print(name, "EOF ok")
print("c2 connected:", c2.connected())

import socket, time, threading
from pwnlib.tubes.listen import listen
from pwnlib.tubes.remote import remote
from pwnlib.tubes.tube import tube
from pwnlib.tubes.buffer import Buffer
from pwnlib.tubes.mux import TubeMultiplexer, MuxChannel

# Buffer watermarks
b = Buffer()
print("initial high/low", b.high_water, b.low_water, b.over_high_water, b.under_low_water)
b.set_watermarks(high=10, low=5)
b.add(b'abcdef')
print("size6 over_high", b.over_high_water, "under_low", b.under_low_water)
b.add(b'ghij')
print("size10 over_high", b.over_high_water, "under_low", b.under_low_water)
b.add(b'k')
print("size11 over_high", b.over_high_water)
b.get(6)
print("size5 under_low", b.under_low_water, "over_high", b.over_high_water)
b.get()
print("size0 under_low", b.under_low_water)
try:
    b.set_watermarks(high=10, low=20)
    print("ERROR no ValueError")
except ValueError:
    print("set_watermarks ValueError ok")

# Constructor validation
l = listen(0); r = remote('localhost', l.lport); srv = l.wait_for_connection()
try:
    TubeMultiplexer("not a tube")
    print("ERROR TypeError")
except TypeError:
    print("TypeError ok")
for bad in [0, -1, 65536, 1.5, "x", True]:
    try:
        TubeMultiplexer(r, max_channels=bad)
        print("ERROR ValueError for", bad)
    except ValueError:
        print("ValueError ok for max_channels", bad)
    except Exception as e:
        print("wrong exc", bad, type(e).__name__, e)
try:
    TubeMultiplexer(r, high_water_mark=10, low_water_mark=20)
    print("ERROR ValueError water")
except ValueError:
    print("water ValueError ok")

m = TubeMultiplexer(r)
print("mux on tube?", isinstance(r.mux(), TubeMultiplexer))
print("properties", m.high_water_mark, m.low_water_mark, m.max_channels, isinstance(m.channels, dict))
m.close()

# MuxChannel subclass
print("MuxChannel subclass of tube:", issubclass(MuxChannel, tube))
r.close(); l.close()
